A ten-character alphabetic CVV was accepted and stored, and a 400-character one produced `500` once its ciphertext exceeded `VARCHAR(512)` — a client cannot act on either outcome. The DTO is the right layer because the CVV has no ordered legacy edit to displace (`COCRDUPC` never edits it: the map could not carry an invalid value) | The rejection message is new text for an input the 3270 map could not express `[app/cpy/CVACT02Y.cpy:L7]` |
| A blank, absent or all-zeros embossed name emits `Card name not provided`, and only non-alphabetic content emits `Card name can only contain alphabets and spaces` (Q14) | Keep one message for both conditions | `COCRDUPC 1230-EDIT-NAME` sets `WS-PROMPT-FOR-NAME` (`'Card name not provided'`) for SPACES, LOW-VALUES and ZEROS, and uses the alphabetic literal only for non-alpha content. Collapsing the two returned the wrong verbatim literal, which the spec-literal fidelity rule forbids | None; both literals are byte-identical to the source `[app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L181-184, L817]` |
| The card-list `accountId` filter is ALWAYS honoured; a non-admin session's own account is applied as an ADDITIONAL restriction, and a non-admin with no account in scope and no filter receives no rows (Q15) | Keep overriding the filter with the session account; return `403` for a foreign account; keep widening an unfiltered non-admin browse to `findAll()` | `COCRDLIC 9500-FILTER-RECORDS` filters on the `ACCTSID` the operator typed and contains NO user-type branch at all (0 hits for `CDEMO-USRTYP-ADMIN`), so overriding the filter was an unrequested behaviour change (AAP §0.7.6) — and it disclosed data: a `ROLE_USER` asking for account 1 received seven cards belonging to OTHER accounts, and once an account was pinned, that account's card instead. Returning the empty-result banner rather than `403` also avoids confirming whether a foreign account exists. Handing a non-admin the entire card base was the same disclosure in its widest form, so a non-admin must now name an account or reach the screen through its own | A non-admin with no account in scope sees `NO RECORDS FOUND FOR THIS SEARCH CONDITION.` until it supplies a filter; the unrestricted browse remains an administrator capability `[app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:L1386]` |

### 11.3 Error Semantics, Precision & Batch Submission

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| Only a unique/primary-key violation naming the transaction id is reported as `Tran ID already exist...`; every other integrity violation propagates to the data-access handler (Q16) | Keep the blanket `catch (DataIntegrityViolationException)`; map each constraint name to its own business message | Three unrelated failures — an over-wide merchant id, an over-wide category code and an account balance too large for `NUMERIC(11,2)` — were all reported as a duplicate transaction id, which invites an endless client retry of a request that can never succeed and hides the real fault. The detector reads the chained `SQLException`'s SQL state (`23505`) and requires the message to name `transactions_pkey`/`tran_id`, so a not-null or check violation mentioning the column is never mistaken for a duplicate | A genuine duplicate reported by a driver that chains no `SQLException` falls back to a text match requiring both a duplicate/unique indication and the key name `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl; app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L535-537]` |
| `tranCatCd` (0–9999) and `tranMerchantId` (0–999999999) are bounded by the SERVICE edits, not by DTO bean validation (Q16) | Add `@Max`/`@Digits` to the DTO; rely on the database `CHECK` constraints | `TCATCDI` is four characters and `MIDI` nine (`PIC 9(04)` / `PIC 9(09)`), so an over-wide value could never be typed on the map. A DTO constraint would be evaluated during argument resolution — BEFORE the handler — and would therefore pre-empt the earlier legacy empty-field edits, replacing verbatim literals such as `Type CD can NOT be empty...` with a field error and changing the observable message ORDER that `COTRN02C`'s `EVALUATE` sequence fixes. Extending the existing `< 0` edits keeps both the literal and its position | The messages reuse the legacy `must be Numeric...` literals for a width violation, which is the closest legacy expression of "this value does not fit the field" `[app/cpy/CVTRA05Y.cpy; app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L325-351, L430-432]` |
| A transaction amount with more than two decimal places is REJECTED with `Amount should be in format -99999999.99`, never rounded into the picture (Q17) | Keep the `HALF_UP` normalisation; reject only on magnitude | `COTRN02C` tests `TRNAMTI(1:1)` for the sign, `(2:8)` numeric, `(10:1)` = `.` and `(11:2)` numeric: a third decimal digit has nowhere to go on the map. Accepting `1.005` and storing `1.01` altered a financial value the caller never authorised, which AAP §0.7.6 forbids, and the account service already rejected scale > 2 — the two services disagreed. Presentation-only trailing zeros (`1.500`) are stripped before the significant scale is measured, so they remain acceptable | A caller that relied on lenient rounding now receives the legacy amount-format literal `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L339-351]` |
| `run.id` is IDENTIFYING for repeatable read/print runs (transaction-detail report, category-balance report, the four data-read jobs, combine-transactions, daily-transaction validation) and stays NON-identifying for state-changing runs (interest calculation) (Q18) | Keep it non-identifying everywhere; make it identifying everywhere; add a per-request business parameter such as a submission timestamp | With identity derived from the business parameters alone, the report WINDOW became the instance key: the monthly report could be printed once per calendar month and the yearly once per year — forever — so one of the seventeen required UI workflows was permanently unusable after its first run. `CORPT00C` writes a TDQ `'JOBS'` record on EVERY request and JES runs the stream each time, so a print or read is a repeatable operation by construction. Making it identifying everywhere was rejected because a duplicate interest-calculation or posting run would then be accepted, and that protection is worth keeping for a job that writes financial rows. A submission timestamp is the same mechanism with a coarser clock | Duplicate detection no longer applies to read/print runs — deliberately. Two IDENTICAL statement runs launched within the same second collide on their shared output file and the loser is recorded `FAILED` ("Unable to create file"); the legacy analogue is JES `DISP=OLD` dataset serialisation. A running-twin guard was rejected because it would make "print the report twice in a row" non-deterministic, which is precisely the operation this fix restores `[app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L450; app/proc/TRANREPT.prc; app/jcl/CREASTMT.JCL]` |
| A report submission REFUSED by batch-service reports the refusal reason (`Report submission was not accepted: …`); only a hand-off that never landed keeps the frozen `Unable to Write TDQ (JOBS)...` (Q18) | Keep the frozen literal for every failure; return the raw upstream status | The frozen literal means one specific thing — the TDQ write itself failed — so using it for a run batch-service answered and refused told the user the wrong cause. The distinction is now the HTTP outcome itself: an answered request is a refusal (reason surfaced from the shared error envelope), while no answer at all is a failed hand-off (frozen literal retained, verbatim) | The refusal text is new observable output, requested by the finding; the frozen literal keeps its exact original meaning `[app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L450]` |

### 11.4 Log Hygiene & Declarative Integrity

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| Every LOG path that carries a request URI masks PAN-shaped digit runs — the access log (`RequestLoggingFilter`), the data-access, optimistic-lock, constraint-violation and protected-data paths of `GlobalExceptionHandler`, the container error dispatch (`CardDemoErrorController`) and the gateway's upstream-failure handler — while RESPONSE bodies keep the URI the caller supplied (Q19) | Mask in the logging framework with a Logback converter or a JSON-layout rewriter; mask the response `path` field too; log a request id instead of the URI | The card screens address a card BY its number, so logging the URI verbatim wrote a full PAN into the access log of every card request and of the gateway in front of it (CWE-532), while the domain-error path was already masking — the inconsistency was visible in the same log file. Masking at each call site keeps the masking decision next to the value and cannot be defeated by a logging-configuration change. The response body legitimately echoes the path the caller itself sent: it is part of the shared error contract and discloses nothing the caller does not already hold. `SensitiveDataMasker` matches 12–19 digit runs, so the 11-digit account id and every shorter identifier stay intact for support | A new log statement that interpolates a URI must mask it; the masker's lower bound leaves shorter identifiers readable by design `[app/cpy/CVACT02Y.cpy; docs/decision-log.md §3]` |
| `transactions.tran_card_num` gains the foreign key `fk_transactions_card` → `cards(card_num)` in `V8__transactions_card_fk.sql`, while `daily_transactions` deliberately keeps none (Q21) | Leave the reference application-enforced and document the omission; constrain against `card_xref` instead; add `ON DELETE CASCADE` | `V1` omitted the key on the grounds that "the posting job must be able to record a transaction whose card is unknown (CBTRN02C reject code 100)", and that reason does not hold: `CBTRN02C` posts a transaction ONLY when validation passed, and reject code 100 is exactly the branch that writes the record to DALYREJS INSTEAD of the transaction master. A posted row therefore always names a resolved card, so the constraint records an invariant both the batch and online paths already maintain — and AAP §0.1.1/§0.4.5 require integrity to be declarative. The UNVALIDATED feed keeps its freedom, because an unknown card arriving on `daily_transactions` is precisely what reject code 100 exists to report. `RESTRICT` (the default) is deliberate: a card carrying posted financial history must not be deletable | Card deletion now fails while transactions reference the card — the protection the constraint exists to provide. Verified against every seeded and migrated database: zero orphan rows, so the constraint validates without a data fix `[app/cbl/CBTRN02C.cbl:L210-215, L380-385; app/cpy/CVTRA05Y.cpy; app/cpy/CVTRA06Y.cpy]` |
| The account update's single `version` token is advanced explicitly when only CUSTOMER fields changed (Q8) | Add a second `custVersion` token to the request; use `OPTIMISTIC_FORCE_INCREMENT` on the account; version the customer independently on the wire | A customer-only edit left the account row untouched, so its version never moved and a second writer holding the same token overwrote the first writer's customer change while receiving `200`. A second token was rejected because the frozen frontend contract declares exactly one `version` on the account update. `OPTIMISTIC_FORCE_INCREMENT` was rejected because it can advance the counter twice for one update, which committed tests pin to a single increment | The one token now moves for any committed change to either half of the aggregate, so a client caching version numbers must re-read after a customer-only edit `[app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl:L4066-4090, L4131-4189; frontend/src/types/account.ts]` |
| The account update edits the remaining legacy field WIDTHS (`acctGroupId` 10, `custAddrLine1`/`2`/`3` 50, names 25, country code 3, government id 20, EFT account id 10, active status 1) inside `AccountUpdateValidator`, in the legacy `1200-EDIT-MAP-INPUTS` order (Q9) | Add `@Size` constraints to the DTO; rely on the column widths | An over-wide value reached the UPDATE and returned `500`. The validator is the right layer for exactly the reason §11.3 gives for the transaction codes: DTO constraints are evaluated before the handler and would displace the ordered verbatim literals this validator exists to reproduce. A latent defect found while doing so is also fixed: the numeric-zero edit parsed a ten-digit `CUST-EFT-ACCOUNT-ID` with `Integer.parseInt` and threw `NumberFormatException` (`500`) on a legal value, where the legacy edit is a character comparison against `ZEROS` | The width messages are new text for inputs the 3270 map could not express; the ordered legacy literals are unchanged `[app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl:L1200-1290; app/cpy/CVCUS01Y.cpy; app/cpy/CVACT01Y.cpy]` |

## 12. Shared Screen-Shell Rendering (`Layout` checkpoint)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The line-23 / line-24 shell state (`transactionId`, `programName`, `title01`, `title02`, `errorMessage`, `infoMessage`, `pfKeys`) lives in `Layout`'s own React state and is published by each page through the `useScreenChrome` context, whose default value is a safe no-op | Require a separate `ScreenChromeProvider` in `App.tsx` / `main.tsx`; lift the chrome into the session store next to `SessionContext`; pass it down as props through every route element | The COMMAREA carried the screen context (`CDEMO-LAST-MAP` / `CDEMO-LAST-MAPSET`, `PIC X(7)`) alongside the user identity, but only the identity has to survive a page reload, so the chrome is per-mount UI state while the session stays in the externalized store. Keeping the provider inside `Layout` means the frozen consumer contract is just `<Layout>{routes}</Layout>` with no extra wiring, and a no-op default keeps `useScreenChrome()` from throwing when a page is rendered standalone in a unit test. Deliberately EXCLUDED from the chrome: a `fieldErrors` member (per-field red / `*` highlighting stays with the page via the `ErrorBanner` helpers, mirroring `CSSETATY`, because it is per-input, not per-screen) and a reset-on-sign-out `useEffect` (it would race a newly mounted page's chrome-setup effect and blank the header the page had just published) | Chrome is not restored across a browser reload; a page that never publishes chrome renders an empty header and key bar, which is the same blank frame the BMS map would send `[app/cpy/COCOM01Y.cpy:L18-45; app/bms/COSGN00.bms]` |
| The never-matching heading reset in `frontend/src/index.css` is repaired by ADDING a SECOND rule `h1.appHeader__center, h2.appHeader__center { margin:0; font:inherit; font-weight:normal }` — typography only, with the original descendant rule left byte-identical and `color: inherit` deliberately NOT repeated | Merge the matching selectors into the existing block (so they also carry `color: inherit`); move the `appHeader__center` class onto a wrapper element so the descendant selector matches; change `Header.tsx`'s markup; leave the rule as-is | The stylesheet requires the two title headings to render as plain 3270 title text with no default browser margin, size, or bold weight, but `.appHeader__center h1, .appHeader__center h2` is a DESCENDANT selector while `Header.tsx` puts the class ON the heading (`<h1 className="appHeader__center title">`), so the rule never applied: the browser defaults were measured instead (`h1` 32px/700 with 21.44px block margins, `h2` 24px/700 with 19.92px), inflating `header.appHeader` to 155.47px against the 41.6px that two 20.8px terminal lines allow (3.74x) and pushing the body off the 24-row grid. Merging the selectors into the existing block was IMPLEMENTED FIRST AND REJECTED: at specificity (0,1,1) and later in the sheet its `color: inherit` out-cascades `.title` / `.appHeader__center` (0,1,0) `color: var(--term-yellow)`, and `inherit` then resolves up an all-neutral chain to `--term-neutral #e6e6e6` — measured in Chrome as rgb(230,230,230), silently dropping the BMS `COLOR=YELLOW` title semantic. The separate typography-only rule fixes the geometry without touching the color cascade. Editing the markup was rejected because the heading's class list and `data-testid` are a frozen contract asserted by `Header.test.tsx` | Presentational only; no DOM, class name or `data-testid` changes. Verified in Chrome after the fix: header 41.59px (2.00 terminal lines), each row 20.80px, titles 16px / weight 400 / 0px margins and rgb(255,210,26) yellow, body top 49.59px, key bar still flush at the content-box bottom `[app/bms/COSGN00.bms; frontend/src/components/Header.tsx]` |

## 13. Frontend Shared Screen Shell & Presentation Test Seams

Decisions taken while delivering the shared 24x80 screen shell (`frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx`),
its colocated suite (`Layout.test.tsx`), the hook barrel (`frontend/src/hooks/index.ts`) and the
session test seam that the suite drives.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `Layout.test.tsx` imports the `jest` object explicitly (`import { jest } from '@jest/globals';`) instead of relying on a bare `jest` global | Reproduce the supplied snippet unchanged and accept the failure; replace `jest.fn()` with a hand-rolled `() => undefined` stub; abandon native ESM and switch the suite to the CJS transform | The supplied canonical content was validated in a CJS-style Jest 29 sandbox where `jest` is injected as a global. This project runs Jest 30 in NATIVE ESM (`"type": "module"`, preset `ts-jest/presets/default-esm`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `transform` with `useESM`), which injects `describe`/`it`/`expect` but NOT `jest`; a probe test confirmed `typeof globalThis.jest === 'undefined'`, so the unmodified snippet raises `ReferenceError: jest is not defined` and the suite cannot reach its own required outcome. The two sibling suites in the same directory already resolve this identically, with the same two-line comment, so the import reuses an established repository pattern rather than introducing one. A hand-rolled stub was rejected because the PF-key handler is a spy the `PFKeyDef` contract types as `() => void` and Jest's own double is the idiomatic form; changing the transform was rejected because it would alter the whole module's test infrastructure to accommodate one file. Every other character of the supplied content is reproduced byte-for-byte — verified by `diff`, whose only hunk is this three-line insertion | The suite now depends on `@jest/globals`, which is part of the Jest distribution already installed; no new dependency `[frontend/src/components/Header.test.tsx:L12-L14; frontend/src/components/PFKeyBar.test.tsx:L14-L16; frontend/package.json]` |
| `useScreenChrome()` called outside a `Layout` returns an INERT context (a no-op `setChrome` over an empty chrome) rather than throwing | Throw "useScreenChrome must be used within a Layout"; return `undefined` and force every caller to null-check; log a warning | A page must stay renderable on its own — in a unit test, or wherever a single screen is mounted without the frame — exactly as a BMS map could be sent without the surrounding shell. Throwing would make every future page component untestable in isolation unless each test re-created the provider, converting a shared convenience into a hard coupling. Returning `undefined` pushes the same problem into every call site and breaks the destructuring form the shell's own contract uses (`const { setChrome } = useScreenChrome()`). A warning was rejected outright: unrequested observable output is forbidden by the spec-literal fidelity rule | Chrome published by a detached page is silently discarded, so a page that renders correctly in isolation is not by itself evidence that its header and PF keys reach the frame; the shell's own suite covers that path `[app/bms/COSGN00.bms; app/cpy/COCOM01Y.cpy:L44]` |
| **SUPERSEDED (§37).** The `__setSession(user, role)` seam is a named export on the production module `frontend/src/hooks/useSession.ts` | Export the private `setState`; put the seam in a separate test-only module; drive authentication by stubbing `sessionStorage` before import; mock the whole `useSession` module per test | The store is deliberately provider-free — module-level state read through `useSyncExternalStore` — so there is no provider a test can wrap to place the SPA in an authenticated state, and the only other route in is `signIn`, which performs a network call the shell's suite has no reason to exercise. Stubbing storage only works before first import and cannot flip state mid-test, which is precisely what the `data-authenticated` assertion needs. Mocking `useSession` would test the mock instead of the real `isAuthenticated` derivation. The seam therefore delegates to the existing private `setState` and builds the same `SessionContext` shape `signIn` already produces | Reversed by §37: the "separate test-only module" alternative dismissed here as unreachable IS reachable — it drives the store through the public `useSession().refresh` rather than through the private `setState` — and the residual risk this row accepted ("a production module carries an export that application code must never call") turned out to be the load-bearing one, because with the seam in place no suite could fail if the SPA's session bootstrap or its route guards were deleted `[app/cpy/COCOM01Y.cpy:L18-L45; app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:L227]` |
| `Layout` publishes the React state setter itself as `setChrome`, narrowed to `(next: ScreenChrome) => void` | Wrap it in a `useCallback`; expose the full `Dispatch<SetStateAction<ScreenChrome>>`; merge partial updates instead of replacing | React guarantees the state setter is identity-stable across renders, so publishing it directly is what makes `useEffect(..., [setChrome])` — the form a page uses to publish its chrome — run exactly once instead of looping. A `useCallback` wrapper would add an indirection with no additional guarantee. The narrowed signature is the whole surface a page needs and keeps the updater form out of the public contract. Replace-not-merge matches the legacy model, where each program moved its OWN complete set of fields into the shared symbolic map before `SEND MAP` rather than inheriting the previous screen's values | A page must publish every field it wants shown in one call; a partial publish clears the omitted fields, which is the intended screen-to-screen behavior `[app/cpy/COCOM01Y.cpy:L18-L45]` |
| `Layout` renders `Header`, `ErrorBanner` and `PFKeyBar` UNCONDITIONALLY, with a shared empty PF-key legend when a screen declares none | Render each region only once a page has published a value for it; let each page render its own header and key bar | The BMS frame is fixed: rows 1-3, line 23 and line 24 exist on every mapset whether or not the program moved anything into them, and `ErrorBanner` already reserves its line with a hidden placeholder so the body does not shift when a message appears. Conditional regions would reflow the 24-row grid between screens. The empty legend is a single module-level constant, so `PFKeyBar` does not rebind its document keydown listener on every render of a screen that declares no keys | The frame occupies its rows even on a screen with no message and no function keys, which is what the 3270 layout did `[app/bms/COSGN00.bms:L26-L28; frontend/src/index.css]` |
| This tranche also delivers `Layout.tsx` and `hooks/index.ts`, not only the assigned suite | Deliver the suite alone and leave the two modules to whichever pass produces them | Both are declared dependencies of the suite and both were absent from the working tree, so the module could neither typecheck nor run its tests without them — an untestable deliverable. Their observable surface is fully determined by the contracts already on disk (`HeaderProps`, `ErrorBannerProps`, `PFKeyDef`, the `.screen` / `.errorBanner` / `.pfKeyBar` classes in `index.css`) plus the assertions the suite makes, so no shape was invented. `frontend/src/main.tsx` and `frontend/src/pages/` were deliberately NOT created for the same test: they are not dependencies of this suite and their contracts are not determined here, so `vite build` still cannot resolve the SPA entry — a documented, pre-existing gap owned by the pass that delivers the pages | Two modules arrive with the suite that exercises them; both are additive, and the module's full suite passes with no regression `[app/bms/COSGN00.bms; app/cpy/CSSTRPFY.cpy; app/cpy/CSSETATY.cpy]` |

## 14. SPA Screen Decisions — Main Menu (`MainMenuPage`, mapset `COMEN01`)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The main-menu option lines bind to `MenuOption.optionName` | Bind to a `label` field as the screen brief sketched | The delivered wire contract (`frontend/src/types/menu.ts`, mirroring `MenuController.MenuOptionView`) declares `optionNumber` / `optionName` / `programName` / `targetRoute` and has no `label` member, and the live gateway response confirms it; the contract is frozen, so the screen binds the field that exists | None: a rename of the DTO field would break the gateway contract first, and TypeScript fails the build if it ever changes `[app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy; frontend/src/types/menu.ts]` |
| Header title lines are the literals `CardDemo` / `Main Menu`, not values read from the menu response | Extend `MenuResponseDto` with `title01` / `title02`; reuse the copybook titles `      AWS Mainframe Modernization       ` / `              CardDemo                  ` | `GET /menu` returns only `tranId`, `programName`, `options` and `message`, so there is no server title to read, and widening the DTO for presentation text would change a delivered API shape. The screen brief fixes the two literals, so they are published verbatim into the shell chrome | The two header title lines differ from `CCDA-TITLE01` / `CCDA-TITLE02`; if the titles must become copybook-exact, the change belongs in the shared header contract, not in one screen `[app/cpy/COTTL01Y.cpy; api-gateway MenuController.MenuResponse]` |
| Option selection navigates from a client-side option-number → route table (`MAIN_MENU_TARGETS`) holding each row's legacy program name and user type | `POST /menu/select` and follow the returned `targetRoute`; navigate straight to `MenuOption.targetRoute` | The gateway resolves one route per SERVICE (`COACTVWC` and `COACTUPC` both yield `/accounts`, the three card programs all yield `/cards`), which cannot address the distinct account-view / account-update / card-view / card-update screens the SPA renders. The screen therefore keeps the ten `CDEMO-MENU-OPTIONS` rows and their per-screen routes, and falls back to the server's `targetRoute` for any option the table does not cover | The table must stay in step with the copybook and with the route table in `App.tsx`; the retained `programName` and `userType` columns make each row auditable against `COMEN02Y` `[app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy; app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L146-L155]` |
| Option → route values: 1 `/accounts`, 2 `/accounts/update`, 3 `/cards`, 4 `/cards/view`, 5 `/cards/update`, 6 `/transactions`, 7 `/transactions/view`, 8 `/transactions/add`, 9 `/reports`, 10 `/billpay` | Encode an identifier in the route (`/accounts/:acctId`); give every screen an explicit verb suffix (`/accounts/view`) | Each feature's first screen keeps the bare service path the gateway already uses, and secondary screens of the same feature take an action suffix. The legacy `XCTL` targets carry no key — `COACTVWC`, `COCRDSLC` and `COTRN01C` each prompt for their identifier on their own screen — so the menu navigates to the entry screen and fabricates no identifier | A route rename in `App.tsx` must be mirrored here; every value was exercised in a browser against the live gateway `[app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L146-L155; api-gateway MenuController.PROGRAM_ROUTES]` |
| The option number renders unpadded (`1. Account View`, `10. Bill Payment`) | Reproduce the 3270 `PIC 9(02)` zero padding (`01. Account View`) | The zero padding is an artefact of a fixed-width display field, and the whole delivered chain models the number as an integer (`MenuOptions.MenuOption.optionNumber` is an `int`, `MenuOption.optionNumber` is a `number`), so the natural rendering carries the same information without re-introducing a storage detail. The visible line keeps the legacy `<number>. <label>` shape | A pixel-exact 3270 comparison shows one fewer leading character on options 1-9 `[app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L243-L246; carddemo-common MenuOptions]` |
| The option list is truncated to twelve lines | Render every option the server returns | The mapset defines exactly twelve option slots, `OPTN001`..`OPTN012`, and the copybook table itself `OCCURS 12 TIMES`, so twelve is the screen's real capacity | An eventual thirteenth option would be silently unreachable from this screen, exactly as on the 3270 `[app/bms/COMEN01.bms:L80-L139; app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy:L88]` |
| While the option list is loading the screen sets `aria-busy` and shows no additional text | Render a "Loading…" line or a spinner | The 3270 screen has no loading state — the map arrives complete — so any placeholder copy would be observable output the legacy program never produced. `aria-busy` conveys the same state to assistive technology with no visual change | A sighted user sees an empty option area for the duration of the fetch (measured at 9 ms against the local gateway) |
| The instructional prompt is a plain `span`, and the entry field is named solely by `aria-label="Option"` | Make the prompt a `<label for>` for the field | The mapset holds the prompt and the entry field as two independent fields, and the screen brief fixes the accessible name as `Option`; pairing a `<label>` reading `Please select an option :` with that name would leave the visible label outside the accessible name (WCAG 2.5.3) | The visible prompt is not programmatically associated with the field; the field still has a correct, unique accessible name `[app/bms/COMEN01.bms:L140-L149]` |
| The entry field carries no `id`, `name` or `autocomplete` attribute | Add `name="option"` to clear the browser's autofill advisory | The field contract is fixed by the screen brief (`className`, `inputMode`, `maxLength`, `aria-label`, value and change handler); adding form-identity attributes would extend the frozen DOM surface for a hint that affects autofill only, and the field is already correctly named and keyboard-operable | Chrome reports an "id or name" DevTools advisory on this field; nothing functional or assistive depends on it |
| The screen body is laid out in normal document flow | Position each element on its absolute 24×80 grid row (heading row 4, options rows 6-17, prompt row 20) | Absolute row placement would need new rules in the shared `frontend/src/index.css` or inline styles, and the delivered shell (`Layout`, `Header`, `ErrorBanner`, `PFKeyBar`) already re-expresses the terminal in flow layout. Field order, labels, grouping and the BMS colour semantics are preserved | The prompt line sits directly below the last option instead of four grid rows below it `[app/bms/COMEN01.bms; docs/decision-log.md §4]` |
| PF3 clears the local session and routes to `/signon` | Call a server-side sign-out first; route to `/signon` without clearing anything | `RETURN-TO-SIGNON-SCREEN` transfers to `COSGN00C` and discards the COMMAREA, and the delivered `useSession.signOut` is a local-session clear by contract, so this is the faithful equivalent | The server-side `SESSION` cookie survives PF3, and `POST /auth/signon` currently answers `500` when a session already exists — an auth-service / gateway idempotency defect recorded here because this screen is where it surfaces `[app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L170-L177; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.ts]` |

## 15. Card-List Screen (`COCRDLI` → `CardListPage`) — SPA Presentation Deviations

Non-literal choices made while delivering the card-list screen of the SPA presentation layer. Each is recorded here per the Explainability rule so the screen stays auditable against `app/bms/COCRDLI.bms` and `app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl`.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| **REVISED.** The card list pages on the SERVER: `pageBackward` / `pageForward` re-issue `GET /cards` with the target `page` and the originating AID (`PF7` / `PF8`), and the screen renders the page the service answered with rather than slicing a fetched set client-side | Slice one fetched result set at a client-side page size of 7 with a shared `usePagination` hook (the shape recorded in the superseded form of this row) | Client-side slicing was only tenable while `CardListResponseDto` carried no paging members. It now publishes `pageNumber` (one-based) and `nextPage`, and `CardService` reads a `PageRequest.of(page - 1, MAX_SCREEN_LINES + 1)` window and returns at most `MAX_SCREEN_LINES = 7` rows — so the service, not the client, knows both which page is displayed and whether another exists. Server paging is also the only shape that reproduces the boundary banners: `COCRDLIC` reaches the program on EVERY declared AID, its `CA-FIRST-PAGE` branch re-reads the same page and `1400-SETUP-MESSAGE` publishes `NO PREVIOUS PAGES TO DISPLAY`, so the AID must travel with the browse and the service must decide the message. Slicing locally would have to invent those banners | One round trip per PF7 / PF8 keystroke instead of none; the 3270 held its whole browse window in the COMMAREA, so this is a deliberate divergence in mechanism with identical observable output. Pinned by `CardListPage.test.tsx`, which asserts the re-fetch count for every paging path `[app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:L1122-L1180; card-service CardService.MAX_SCREEN_LINES; frontend/src/pages/CardListPage.tsx]` |
| `S` is the only accepted row-action code, and the info line `TYPE S FOR DETAIL, U TO UPDATE ANY RECORD` is not published (`infoMessage` stays empty) | Also accept `U` and transfer to the card-update screen, publishing the info line | The screen contract for `CardListPage` enumerates exactly one action — `S` opens the card detail — and pins `infoMessage` to empty; emitting an unrequested info line, or an unrequested `U` transfer, would breach spec-literal fidelity. Card update remains reachable from the card-detail screen | A user who types `U` now receives `INVALID ACTION CODE` instead of the update screen `[app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:L1079-L1115]` |
| At the first / last page the PF7 / PF8 keys are DISABLED instead of emitting `NO PREVIOUS PAGES TO DISPLAY` / `NO MORE PAGES TO DISPLAY` | Keep both keys always active and publish the two boundary literals | The shared `PFKeyBar` gates a key on its `enabled` flag, so the boundary is unreachable; publishing a message for an outcome the user can no longer trigger would be unrequested observable output. The two literals remain reachable through the `card-service` route for API clients | The two boundary literals never appear in the SPA `[app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl 1400-SETUP-MESSAGE]` |
| **RESOLVED.** The one-character row-action field keeps the mapset's single-character VALUE and `size=1`, and the shared stylesheet enlarges only its HIT AREA to the 24×24 CSS-pixel minimum (WCAG 2.5.8, AA) with the character centred in it | Leave the hit area at the one-cell size and flag the shortfall for review (the position an earlier revision of this row recorded); enlarge the control itself to 24×24; enlarge it to 44×44 | The BMS mapset is the design source — `CRDSELn` is a `PIC X(1)` field occupying one 3270 cell — so the VALUE and the `size` attribute cannot change. But the hit area is not the mapset's concern: `input[maxlength="1"], input[size="1"]` in `frontend/src/index.css` sets `min-width`/`min-height` to `24px` and centres the glyph, which satisfies 2.5.8 without altering the 80-column character grid or the rendered value. Flagging the shortfall was therefore the wrong call: the requirement and the grid were never actually in conflict. 44×44 is the AAA-level 2.5.5 target and is not the bar this project holds | The same rule covers the `COTRN00` selection column and the `COBIL00`/`CORPT00`/`COTRN02`/`COCRDUP` confirm fields, and the sibling rule on `button` applies the same 24px minimum height — so a future one-character field inherits the target size rather than needing its own rule. An earlier revision of this row claimed the field measured ≈27×23 px, fell BELOW the 24×24 minimum, and carried a `BLITZY [A11Y]` flag in code; the stylesheet meets the minimum and no source file carries such a flag, so all three statements were false and are corrected here `[frontend/src/index.css:L190-L202; app/bms/COCRDLI.bms:L140-L323]` |
| The card filter travels to `card-service` as the `cardNumber` query parameter, while the client-side DTO field keeps its `cardNum` spelling | Send `cardNum` (the DTO spelling) on the wire; or rename the DTO field to `cardNumber` | `CardController.listCards` binds `@RequestParam("cardNumber")`, so a `cardNum` parameter was silently discarded and the `CARDSID` filter of `2220-EDIT-CARD` never reached the browse; renaming the DTO field instead would break the frozen `CardListRequestDto` contract already consumed by the SPA | Only `api/cards.ts` knows the wire name, so the mapping must be kept in step with the controller `[app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:L1036-L1069; card-service CardController.listCards]` |
| `jest-environment-jsdom` is given `TextEncoder` / `TextDecoder` from `node:util` in `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` | Leave the polyfill out and skip router-dependent tests; or replace the Jest environment | jsdom exposes neither global, yet `react-router` v7 constructs a `TextEncoder` while it is being imported, so EVERY routed-screen test aborted with `ReferenceError: TextEncoder is not defined`. The guarded polyfill is test-environment-only and changes no application behaviour | None for production code; the shim is only evaluated by `setupFilesAfterEnv` `[frontend/src/setupTests.ts]` |

## 16. SPA Presentation — Card Detail Screen (`COCRDSL` / `CCDL` / `COCRDSLC`)

Decisions taken while building `frontend/src/pages/CardDetailPage.tsx`, the React replacement for BMS mapset `app/bms/COCRDSL.bms`. Every row is a choice a competent engineer could reasonably have made differently, so it is recorded here rather than in a code comment, per the Explainability rule (AAP §0.7.2).

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| Authored the two declared dependencies that did not yet exist — `frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx` (exporting `ScreenChrome`, `useScreenChrome`, and the `Layout` shell) and the `frontend/src/hooks/index.ts` barrel | Wait for them to appear; import from `../hooks/useApi` directly and inline the chrome regions in the page | Both are AAP §0.4.3 / §0.3.2 CREATE targets and both are declared dependencies of this page, yet `useScreenChrome` existed nowhere in the tree, so the module could not compile or be tested at all. The contract was derived verbatim from the page's own `setChrome({ transactionId, programName, title01, title02, errorMessage, infoMessage, pfKeys })` payload | Additive; a later task authoring the same shell must keep that payload shape and the `useScreenChrome()` → `{ chrome, setChrome }` return `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx]` |
| `useScreenChrome` returns a context default whose `setChrome` is a no-op instead of throwing when no `Layout` is above it | Throw “used outside a Layout provider”, as a strict context hook usually does | A page must render standalone — in isolation tests and when mounted outside the shell — exactly as it does inside it. Throwing would make every page untestable without stubbing the shell, and the chrome is presentational, so its absence is not an error | A page mounted outside the shell silently publishes nowhere; the header/message/PF regions simply do not render `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx]` |
| `setChrome` REPLACES the published chrome and performs no equality short-circuit | Merge partial updates; skip the state write when the incoming chrome looks unchanged | An equality check can only compare PF-key `action`/`label`, never the `onActivate` closures, so a screen whose handler had captured new input state would keep the STALE handler and search on the previous value — a real lost-input defect. Replacement keeps what renders exactly equal to what the active page published | Every publish re-renders the shell; pages must publish from an effect with primitive dependencies, which this page does `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx]` |
| The `ACCTSID` account filter is validated only when supplied; the 16-digit `CARDSID` is required, and the account edit is evaluated FIRST so its message wins | Require both filters, as `COCRDSLC` `2210-EDIT-ACCOUNT` does for the 3270 screen; validate the card first | The migrated read key is the card number (`9100-GETCARD-BYACCTCARD` moves `CC-CARD-NUM` to the record id), and the screen is routed as `/cards/view` with the selected card handed over in the router location state, so an entry from the card list carries no account filter and must still display. The frozen literals themselves say “IF SUPPLIED”. Edit ORDER is preserved from `2210-EDIT-ACCOUNT` → `2220-EDIT-CARD` with `WS-RETURN-MSG-OFF` first-message-wins semantics, so an invalid account still masks a card error | A blank account no longer raises `Account number not provided`; that literal, and `Card number not provided` / `No input received`, are not emitted because the interface for this screen enumerates only the four messages implemented — emitting more would be unrequested observable output `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L620-L720]` |
| Displays `cardEmbossedName` for the `Name on card      :` field | Introduce a `cardName` member to match the requirement's prose | `CardDetailResponseDto` is a frozen contract and declares `cardEmbossedName`; the requirement itself directs that the actual member names in `frontend/src/types/card.ts` are authoritative. It is also the field `1200-SETUP-SCREEN-VARS` moves into `CRDNAMEO` | None; no type was altered `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L476; frontend/src/types/card.ts]` |
| `cardExpiraionDate` is split POSITIONALLY (characters 1-4 year, 6-7 month) with each part independently required to be numeric, rather than parsed as a date | Parse with a date library and reformat; regex-match the whole `YYYY-MM-DD` shape | `CARD-EXPIRAION-DATE-X` is redefined positionally by `CARD-EXPIRY-YEAR` / `CARD-EXPIRY-MONTH`, so positional extraction is what the source does. The per-part numeric guard is what keeps a malformed value from rendering as visible junk while still never “correcting” a legal one | A value whose separators differ still yields the legacy month/year; a non-numeric part renders empty rather than raw `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L84-L92]` |
| The line-23 message is the client edit literal when one applies, otherwise the service's own normalized `ApiError.message`, verbatim | Map HTTP statuses to locally authored messages; show nothing on failure | The card-service already answers with the legacy literals, so passing its message through preserves them end to end without inventing text, and giving the client edit precedence stops a stale error from masking a fresh field edit | A future service message change surfaces directly on the screen, which is the intended single source of that text `[frontend/src/api/client.ts]` |
| No submit button is rendered in the screen body; the shell's line-24 `ENTER=Search Cards` key is the only submit affordance | Render a submit button inside the form (visually hidden, or visible) | The 3270 screen has exactly one ENTER affordance and it lives on line 24, which `PFKeyBar` renders and which also binds the physical Enter key. A second control would duplicate the legend as unrequested observable output | A `<form>` with no default button performs no implicit submission, so Enter is handled by the PF-key binding; verified in the browser for both the button and the physical key `[app/bms/COCRDSL.bms:L148-L152]` |
| An in-flight read is conveyed only by `aria-busy`, and an absent card leaves the display fields blank | Render a “Loading…” indicator and a “No data” placeholder | The mapset emits no such text — it simply leaves the fields unfilled when `FOUND-CARDS-FOR-ACCOUNT` is false — and inventing either string would be unrequested observable output. `aria-busy` carries the state to assistive technology with zero visual change | A slow read shows an unchanged screen; the state is still exposed programmatically `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L474-L485]` |
| Four terminal-fidelity rules were added to the shared `frontend/src/index.css` rather than to a page-local stylesheet: the `main h1/h2/h3` heading reset, `main dl > div { display:flex; gap:1ch }` with `main dl dd { margin-inline-start:0 }`, and `main form label, main dl dt { white-space:pre }` | Introduce a per-page CSS module; accept the browser defaults | Runtime inspection showed the browser defaults contradicted the mapset on three counts: the line-4 title rendered 24px/700 instead of the uniform character cell, each value fell onto its own line with the 40px user-agent `dd` indent although the mapset places caption (column 4) and value (column 25) on ONE row, and `white-space: normal` collapsed the caption padding so the five values landed on four different columns. The rules are generic terminal semantics every screen needs, and the project ships one plain global stylesheet by design (AAP §0.3.5) | Shared scope: all 17 screens inherit them. Measured after the change — captions render an identical 182.4375px and every value shares one left edge (0.015625px, one 1/64px layout snap) `[app/bms/COCRDSL.bms:L103-L136]` |
| Repaired a pre-existing selector defect in `frontend/src/index.css`: added `h1.appHeader__center, h2.appHeader__center` alongside the existing descendant rule, deliberately WITHOUT a `color` declaration | Leave it; change `Header.tsx` to nest the heading inside a `.appHeader__center` wrapper | `.appHeader__center h1, .appHeader__center h2` matched ZERO elements, because `Header.tsx` puts that class ON the heading, so the documented intent (“must still render as plain 3270 title text: no default browser margin, size, or bold weight”) never took effect and the header rendered 32px/700 and 24px/700, occupying 145px instead of two 20.8px rows. The fix realizes the existing comment rather than introducing a new design. `color` is omitted on purpose: the descendant form's `color: inherit` would inherit from the row and turn the titles GREY, whereas the co-applied `.title` keeps them YELLOW | Changing the shell header affects all 17 screens; measured after the change — header 41.59px = 2 × 20.797px, titles still `rgb(255, 210, 26)` `[frontend/src/components/Header.tsx; frontend/src/index.css]` |
| The centered line-4 title is an OPT-IN `.screenTitle` class applied by this page, not a blanket `main h2 { text-align: center }` | Centre every body heading globally; leave the title left-aligned | `COCRDSL` places the literal at `POS(4,30)`, i.e. centered across the 80 columns, but the other sixteen mapsets position their own literals independently, so a global rule would impose this screen's layout on all of them. Measured after the change: text ink centre 640.000px against a body centre of 640.000px — the exact grid centre, which `POS(4,30)` approximates only because column 29.5 must round up | A screen wanting the same treatment must opt in `[app/bms/COCRDSL.bms:L75-L78]` |
| Added conditional `TextEncoder` / `TextDecoder` polyfills to `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` | Stub `react-router-dom` in each page test; switch the Jest environment | `jest-environment-jsdom` ships neither global, and `react-router` reads `TextEncoder` at module load, so EVERY routed page test failed to load with `ReferenceError: TextEncoder is not defined` before a single assertion ran. Node's implementations are spec-compatible, and the install is conditional so a future jsdom that ships its own keeps it | Shared test setup: applies to all suites; the 68 pre-existing tests were re-run unchanged `[frontend/src/setupTests.ts]` |

## 17. Card Update Screen (`CardUpdatePage` checkpoint)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| F5 runs the edit pass FIRST when the changes have not been confirmed yet and, if they are clean, continues straight to the rewrite in the same press | Require the legacy two-press sequence (ENTER validates, only then does F5 save); save whatever is on the screen without re-editing | `COCRDUPC` gates PF05 on `CCUP-CHANGES-OK-NOT-CONFIRMED` and REMAPS every other AID to ENTER (`IF PFK-INVALID SET CCARD-AID-ENTER TO TRUE`), so a first F5 press only edits. Reproducing that literally makes the primary workflow silently do nothing on first press, and the map's own legend advertises `F5=Save`. Saving without re-editing was rejected outright: it is the legacy hole in which `1200-EDIT-MAP-INPUTS` SKIPS the edits while the state is already `CCUP-CHANGES-OK-NOT-CONFIRMED`, so a field edited after validation reaches the rewrite unvalidated. The message sequence is unchanged — a failing edit still publishes its verbatim literal and no request is issued, and ENTER alone still publishes `Changes validated.Press F5 to save` | One fewer keystroke than the 3270 flow; a client that counted screen transmissions sees one less. Values edited AFTER a successful ENTER are still sent as-is on the next F5, preserving the legacy behaviour for that path `[app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L413-L423, L645-L716]` |
| Only the four edits the screen contract enumerates run on the client — card key 16 digits, status `Y`/`N`, month 1-12, year 1950-2099 — while `1230-EDIT-NAME` stays with `card-service` | Reproduce `Card name not provided` / `Card name can only contain alphabets and spaces` on the page as well; drop the name edit entirely | The page's frozen message set is those four literals; adding two more client literals emits observable output the contract does not ask for, which AAP 0.7.2 forbids. The edit itself is NOT lost — `CardService.validateEmbossedName` already reproduces both literals verbatim (traceability matrix Section 17), and this page surfaces the backend `ApiErrorResponse.message` unchanged, so a blank or non-alphabetic name still reports the exact legacy text, just one round trip later | A name edit costs an HTTP round trip instead of failing locally; the literals the user sees are identical `[app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L181-L184, L806-L866]` |
| The screen chrome (`transactionId`, `programName`, titles, message, `pfKeys`) is published from a `useLayoutEffect`, not a `useEffect` | Keep the passive `useEffect`; hold the PF-key handlers in a `useRef` latest-value box so the published array can safely be a generation old; publish through a reducer keyed on a save-request counter | Passive effects are flushed on a scheduled task, so the array handed to `PFKeyBar` — and the line-23 message — lagged one commit behind the fields they act on. Measured with an instrumented probe: immediately after the record was read the DOM showed status `Y` while the published ENTER handler still closed over the pre-fetch empty state, so the first press reported `Card Active Status must be Y or N` for a valid record and issued no rewrite. A layout effect is flushed synchronously during commit, which both closes that window and keeps the BMS `SEND MAP` property that the body, the line-23 message and the line-24 legend reach the screen in ONE transmission rather than the body painting one frame ahead of its own message. The ref box and the reducer both fix the staleness too, but each adds indirection to reach a guarantee the effect ordering already provides | The publish costs one synchronous parent re-render per commit in which the page's own state changed; the tree is a single screen, and no layout is read back so there is no thrash `[app/bms/COCRDUP.bms; app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L1085-L1135]` |
| Every field cell reserves a fixed one-character blank-marker slot (`.cardUpdate__marker { flex: 0 0 1ch }`), including the two fields this screen never flags | Render the marker only when it is non-empty; write the `*` INTO the field value as the legacy program does; drop the marker and rely on the red field colour alone | The marker spans were flex items with a `0.5ch` gap on only three of the five rows, so the entry fields were staggered 4.80px and a rendered `*` would have shifted its row a further character cell — measured in Chrome. Conditional rendering keeps the stagger and adds the shift. Writing `*` into the value is what `COCRDUPC` does (it later reads `EXPMONI = '*'` as "blank"), but on a controlled React input that would replace the operator's own text and submit `*` as the value. A reserved gutter reproduces the observable result — a red `*` immediately left of a blank required field — with every field starting in one terminal column and zero movement when it appears (verified: the input's border occupies the same device columns in both states) | One extra character column between caption and field on every row, applied uniformly; the two never-flagged cells render an empty slot `[app/cpy/CSSETATY.cpy; app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L623-L636]` |
| `CARDSID` renders PROTECTED (read-only), even though the BMS field is defined `ATTRB=(FSET,NORM,UNPROT)` | Make the card number editable and re-read on ENTER, matching the raw mapset definition | The mapset defines the field unprotected because the same map serves the pre-fetch search state, but `3300-SETUP-SCREEN-ATTRS` moves `DFHBMPRF` to `CARDSIDA` for every state in which a record is displayed — the key is protected exactly when this route is showing one. In the SPA the key is handed over in the router location state for `/cards/update` and is the path of the rewrite (`PUT /cards/{cardNumber}`), so editing it in place would silently retarget the update. The card-number edit is still reproduced against the routed key, so a malformed key reports `Card number if supplied must be a 16 digit number` and issues no read | Re-keying to a different card is done by navigating (the card list / detail screens), not by typing over the key `[app/bms/COCRDUP.bms; app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L1172-L1196]` |
| The BMS caption literals keep their internal padding (`Account Number    :`) and are rendered with `white-space: pre` | Trim the padding to a single space and align the columns with the CSS grid alone; substitute `&nbsp;` | The padding IS the 3270 column-alignment mechanism: all five captions are padded to the same width so their colons land in one column, and HTML would collapse the runs to a single space, leaving the colons ragged. `&nbsp;` would align them but changes the DOM text away from the frozen literal (U+00A0 for U+0020). Keeping ASCII spaces plus `white-space: pre` preserves the literal byte-for-byte AND the alignment — verified in Chrome: the colon glyph rect is identical on all five captions. Accessible names are unaffected because name computation collapses whitespace, so assistive technology hears `Account Number :` | A caption query written with the padded literal must allow for the testing-library whitespace normalisation that every DOM text comparison applies `[app/bms/COCRDUP.bms; app/cpy-bms/COCRDUP.CPY]` |
| `EXPMON` accepts one or two digits and is zero-padded to `MM` on the wire | Require two characters; send the value exactly as typed | `1250-EDIT-EXPIRY-MON` tests `VALID-MONTH VALUES 1 THRU 12` over the numeric redefinition of an `X(2)` field whose two characters are filled by the terminal's `JUSTIFY=(RIGHT)` attribute — a 3270 service with no browser equivalent. The stated rule is the range, so `9` is accepted as September and serialised `09`, which keeps `cardExpiraionDate` a valid `YYYY-MM-DD` string; rejecting `9` would enforce a terminal artifact rather than the rule | A single-digit month is accepted where the 3270 relied on the hardware to pad it; the wire format is unchanged `[app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:L92-L99, L887-L910]` |
| `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` installs the Node `TextEncoder` / `TextDecoder` on `globalThis` behind `typeof … === 'undefined'` guards | Polyfill inside each page test file; pin an older router; add a custom Jest environment | jsdom ships no WHATWG encoding API, and react-router 7 constructs one as soon as a router renders, so EVERY page suite died with `ReferenceError: TextEncoder is not defined` before its first assertion — a shared gap, not a per-page one. Per-file polyfills would repeat it seventeen times; downgrading the router contradicts the pinned dependency set; a custom environment adds a build artifact for two globals. The guards keep the assignment inert wherever the runtime already provides them | Test-time only — no production code path and no bundle impact `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/setupTests.ts]` |

## 18. Transaction-View Screen Rendering (`TranViewPage` checkpoint)

> **SUPERSEDED — HISTORICAL RECORD ONLY.** Four of the five rows below were reversed by
> the `TranViewPage` row in §19.3 (findings M-03 / M-20). They are retained so the
> reasoning that was reversed stays auditable, and each reversed row is prefixed
> `SUPERSEDED`; none of them describes the delivered code. What ships is:
> `OutputField` `<dt>`/`<dd>` pairs (zero `readOnly` inputs, so no protected value is a
> tab stop), the BMS caption `Enter Tran ID:`, the `COTTL01Y` header pair
> `CCDA_TITLE01` / `CCDA_TITLE02`, and the verbatim four-key legend
> `ENTER=Fetch  F3=Back  F4=Clear  F5=Browse Tran.`.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `TranViewPage` imports the detail DTO as `TranViewResponseDto`, the name that already exists in `frontend/src/types/transaction.ts`, rather than the `TransactionDetailResponseDto` named in the file's own build instruction | Add a `TransactionDetailResponseDto` alias to `../types`; rename the existing interface across the type layer and the api module | The instruction itself qualifies the name with "use the exact detail DTO name in `../types`", and the frozen contract already ships `TranViewResponseDto` (consumed by `getTransaction` in `frontend/src/api/transactions.ts`). Spec-literal fidelity gives precedence to an EXISTING symbol over a statement spelling of it, so the code's current name is kept and the discrepancy recorded here. An alias would create two names for one contract; a rename would break `api/transactions.ts` and the committed `types/__tests__/contract.test.ts` | None functional — the type is structurally identical either way `[app/cpy-bms/COTRN01.CPY; frontend/src/types/transaction.ts; frontend/src/api/transactions.ts]` |
| **SUPERSEDED (§19.3).** The line-24 legend is exactly two keys, `ENTER=Search` and `F3=Exit`, and F3 navigates to `/transactions` | Reproduce the BMS line-24 literal `ENTER=Fetch  F3=Back  F4=Clear  F5=Browse Tran.` verbatim with all four keys; keep four keys but relabel them; send F3 to the main menu as `COTRN01C` does | `COTRN01.bms` really does declare four keys, and `COTRN01C` maps PF3 to `CDEMO-FROM-PROGRAM` (else `COMEN01C`), PF4 to `CLEAR-CURRENT-SCREEN` and PF5 to `COTRN00C`. The frozen page contract instead specifies a two-key `pfKeys` array with these exact labels and handlers, and a statement that ENUMERATES its members is satisfied only by implementing every member and no more — so adding F4/F5 would be unrequested observable output. F3 → `/transactions` also collapses the legacy PF3/PF5 split into the single sensible SPA target, since the transaction list is the only screen that routes into this one | The 3270 F4=Clear and F5=Browse shortcuts are not reachable on this screen; clearing is achieved by emptying the search field, and the list is one F3 away `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L259-268; app/cbl/COTRN01C.cbl:L113-131]` |
| **SUPERSEDED (§19.3).** The entry-field caption reads `Search Tran ID:`; the header lines carry `title01` `CardDemo` and `title02` `View Transaction` | Use the BMS caption `Enter Tran ID:`; populate the header from `COTTL01Y` (`      AWS Mainframe Modernization       ` / `              CardDemo                  `) | Both values are frozen literals of the page contract, stated as backticked UI copy and as an explicit `setChrome` call. The BMS caption and the `COTTL01Y` title pair are the legacy spellings, recorded here as the deviation they are. The contract's choice is also self-consistent: the key legend says `ENTER=Search`, so naming the field "Search" keeps caption and legend in agreement, and moving the screen name into `title02` makes the header identify the screen rather than the product twice | A user comparing screen-to-screen against a 3270 capture sees three differing strings; every other caption on the map is byte-identical `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L80-90; app/cpy/COTTL01Y.cpy:L17-22]` |
| **SUPERSEDED (§19.3).** Protected (`ATTRB=ASKIP`) detail values render as `<input readOnly>` carrying the `label` class, and every display value is sliced to its BMS `LENGTH=` | Render values as plain `<span>`/`<output>` text; use `disabled` instead of `readOnly`; pass the wire value through untruncated | `readOnly` inputs keep a real `<label for>` association, so all thirteen values resolve by caption for assistive technology and for React Testing Library, which `<span>` cannot offer and `<output>` only offers by taking on an `aria-live` role thirteen times over. `disabled` was rejected because it drops the value from the accessibility tree. The `label` class (specificity 0,1,0) out-cascades the global `input` rule (0,0,1), so the text renders BMS BLUE while the entry field alone stays GREEN — measured in Chrome as rgb(74,144,255) versus rgb(51,255,102). The width slice reproduces what a COBOL `MOVE` into a `PIC X(n)` symbolic-map field does, which is what makes the 26-character `TRAN-ORIG-TS` / `TRAN-PROC-TS` display as the `YYYY-MM-DD` date portion in the `X(10)` `TORIGDT` / `TPROCDT` fields the map declares | A protected field keeps a field box and border that a 3270 protected field would not draw; a value longer than its map width is silently shortened, exactly as the legacy `MOVE` shortened it `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L105-256; app/cbl/COTRN01C.cbl:L177-190; app/cpy-bms/COTRN01.CPY]` |
| **SUPERSEDED (§19.3).** The thirteen read-only fields remain in the tab order (plain `readOnly`, no `tabIndex={-1}`) | Set `tabIndex={-1}` on every protected field so the keyboard skips them as a 3270 `ASKIP` field does | The page contract specifies only "read-only display fields" and says nothing about tab behaviour, and introducing behaviour the statement does not state is a contract violation. The AAP's "tab-order semantics" obligation is met by preserving the BMS field ORDER, which the rendered geometry confirms line for line. Keeping the fields focusable also lets a keyboard user select and copy a value, which is standard web behaviour for read-only inputs | Fourteen Tab presses precede the key bar, so a keyboard user reaches `ENTER=Search` more slowly than a 3270 operator would `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L85-90; AAP 0.3.5]` |
| The displayed message is DERIVED each render as `validationMessage !== '' ? validationMessage : resolveApiMessage(error)`, and a `404` is mapped to the frozen `Transaction ID NOT found...` regardless of the body text | Store one `errorMessage` in state and overwrite it from a `useEffect` that watches the request error; trust the backend's own message for a `404` | `useApi.run` never rejects and records its failure in state, so reading `error` immediately after awaiting `run` yields the previous value; deriving the message removes that ordering hazard entirely and needs no second state variable. The derived precedence also reproduces the legacy control flow, where the blank-id check short-circuits `PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` before `READ-TRANSACT-FILE` is ever performed — verified in Chrome: a blank search shows the empty-id message and issues no request. `transaction-service` already returns exactly `Transaction ID NOT found...` on `404`, so mapping the status makes the frozen literal independent of any future rewording upstream | A backend that starts returning `404` for something other than a missing transaction would still read "Transaction ID NOT found..." `[app/cbl/COTRN01C.cbl:L142-155, L266-296; transaction-service TransactionService MSG_TRAN_ID_NOT_FOUND]` |
| The `X(70)` dashed rule at BMS line 8 becomes `<hr class="neutral">`, and the page introduces the rule-free semantic class names `tranViewPage` / `screenLine` / `detailField` instead of adding rules to `frontend/src/index.css` | Render the seventy literal `-` characters; add a per-screen block to the shared `index.css`; add a `TranViewPage.css` module | `<hr>` is the semantic thematic break, so a screen reader announces a separator instead of spelling out seventy dashes, and it renders as the same horizontal line. Reusing only the existing BMS-colour classes (`prompt`, `label`, `field`, `neutral`) keeps this screen out of the shared stylesheet, which sixteen sibling page components are editing concurrently; `Layout` sets the precedent by using `screenBody` with no rule of its own. A per-page stylesheet was rejected as a file the contract does not call for | The three new class names carry no styling, so a future visual refinement must add rules for them `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L94-99; frontend/src/index.css; frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx]` |
| `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` gains a guarded `TextEncoder` / `TextDecoder` assignment onto `globalThis` from `node:util` | Polyfill inside each page test file; add a second `setupFiles` entry pointing at a new polyfill module; switch the test environment away from jsdom | `jest-environment-jsdom` defines neither global (probed: `TextEncoder`, `TextDecoder`, `ReadableStream`, `TransformStream`, `MessageChannel`, `structuredClone` and `fetch` are all `undefined`), while React Router 7 reads `TextEncoder` at MODULE LOAD. Every one of the seventeen page components needs a Router for `useParams` / `useNavigate`, so without this the whole UI-workflow test surface fails with `ReferenceError` before a single assertion runs. A per-test polyfill CANNOT work: ES modules hoist all static imports, so `react-router-dom` is evaluated before any statement in the test body — the assignment has to happen in `setupFilesAfterEnv`. Both writes are guarded on `undefined`, so nothing jsdom does provide is ever overridden, and only the two globals actually required were added | Adds two lines of environment shimming to a file whose own instruction says to keep it minimal; the instruction's prohibition targets global MOCKS, and this is environment parity, not a mock `[frontend/src/setupTests.ts; frontend/package.json jest.setupFilesAfterEnv]` |

## 19. Cross-Cutting Review Remediation — 17-Screen SPA Checkpoint

Decisions taken while resolving the 46 findings of the `UserDeletePage` review checkpoint across
the whole 17-screen SPA, its API/type layer, the shared shell and the services behind them. Rows
marked **AAP PRECEDENCE** record a finding whose suggested resolution conflicts with the frozen
AAP; in each the AAP is followed and the specific section is cited. Rows marked **SUPERSEDES**
explicitly retire an earlier decision in this log.

### 19.1 Findings declined on AAP precedence

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| **AAP PRECEDENCE.** No per-principal entitlement scoping is added to the account, card, transaction, bill-pay or report read/write paths (findings C-03, C-05, C-06 authorization half) | Filter every read by the signed-on user's own customer id; add an ownership predicate to each repository query; reject a request whose key is not linked to the session principal | The legacy programs authorize by SCREEN REACHABILITY, not by row ownership: `COSGN00C` routes on `SEC-USR-TYPE` to `COADM01C` or `COMEN01C` and every screen the menu offers then operates on any key the operator types. There is no per-row entitlement construct anywhere in the sources — `CVACT01Y`, `CVACT03Y` and `CSUSR01Y` carry no owning-user column, and `COACTVWC`/`COCRDSLC`/`COTRN01C` read on the typed key alone. AAP §0.7.1 requires validation rules and workflow sequencing reproduced EXACTLY, §0.7.6 forbids enhancing business processes, and §0.7.2 Rule 5 forbids unrequested observable behaviour. Adding a scoping predicate would deny requests the legacy grants, changing observable behaviour for unchanged input. Verified across Phases 8-11: zero invented role scoping in any of these pages | A signed-on non-admin can read any account/card/transaction by typing its key, exactly as on the 3270. Mitigated by the role gate on the admin screens (which IS legacy), TLS, and PII masking per §0.6.7 `[app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:L227-L237; app/cpy/CVACT01Y.cpy; app/cpy/CVACT03Y.cpy; AAP §0.7.1, §0.7.6, §0.7.2]` |
| **AAP PRECEDENCE.** The card screens keep the `COCRDSL` two-field composite selection (`ACCTSID` + `CARDSID`) and do not derive an ownership filter from the session (finding C-04 ownership half) | Discard the typed `ACCTSID` and scope the card read to the session's own account set | `COCRDSLC` edits BOTH inputs and reads on the composite key; discarding `ACCTSID` was itself the defect the finding reported in its other half, and that half WAS fixed in Phase 3 by restoring the composite selection. Deriving the account from the session instead would replace one invented rule with another and is bypassable, which the finding itself observed | Same as the row above `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L120-L160; AAP §0.7.1]` |
| **AAP PRECEDENCE.** Bill payment is not given a server-issued confirmation token bound to a displayed amount (finding C-06 token half) | Issue a signed token with the previewed balance and require it on confirm | `COBIL00C` has no amount field to bind: `COBIL00.bms` declares only `ACTIDIN`, the PROTECTED `CURBAL` and the one-character `CONFIRM`. On confirm the program RE-READS the account (`READ-ACCTDAT-FILE`) and posts `TRAN-AMT = the freshly-read ACCT-CURR-BAL`, so the amount is never taken from the screen and a stale display cannot be replayed. The re-read is the legacy's own protection and is reproduced verbatim; a token would guard a value the wire never carries | None identified. A concurrent balance change between preview and confirm posts the NEW balance, which is precisely what the legacy does `[app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L153-L243; app/bms/COBIL00.bms]` |
| **AAP PRECEDENCE.** Card and account detail screens continue to DISPLAY the values the mapset declares, including the full card number (finding C-07, display half) | Mask the PAN on screen; show only the last four digits; hide it behind a reveal control | AAP §0.6.7 enumerates the fields to mask and encrypt — SSN, government id and CVV — and the card PAN is deliberately NOT among them; `COCRDSL`/`COCRDUP` declare `CARDSID`/`CCUPNUM` as displayed fields. §0.3.5 requires field labels and content preserved. Masking would withhold output the frozen mapset paints. The finding's OTHER half — PAN in browser URLs, history entries, ARIA labels and test ids — WAS fixed and browser-verified (`hrefContainsCardNumber:false`; the number travels in navigation state) | The PAN is visible to whoever is at the terminal, as on the 3270. Residual: the PAN remains in the same-origin API path `/cards/{cardNumber}` and the `cardNumber` query parameter; mitigated by nginx logging `$uri` only and by `RequestLoggingFilter` masking PAN-shaped digit runs `[AAP §0.6.7, §0.3.5; app/bms/COCRDSL.bms]` |
| **AAP PRECEDENCE.** No role-based field hiding is added to the account or customer detail screens (finding M-10, data-minimization half) | Hide or redact customer fields for non-admin operators | `COACTVW`/`COACTUP` paint one field set for every operator; `SEC-USR-TYPE` gates which SCREENS the menu offers, never which fields a screen shows. Per-role field hiding is a new business rule, forbidden by §0.7.6. The masking §0.6.7 DOES require (SSN, government id, CVV) is implemented and unconditional | A non-admin sees the same customer fields an admin sees, as on the 3270 `[app/bms/COACTVW.bms; AAP §0.7.6, §0.6.7]` |

### 19.2 Security, dependency and tooling decisions

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `POST /reports/statements` stays gated to `ROLE_ADMIN` (finding M-26 resolved by keeping the restriction) | Open the endpoint to any authenticated user to match `CORPT00C`'s menu reachability | AAP §0.4.2 scopes reporting-service to `POST /reports` (`CR00`) only, and §0.4.4 places `StatementGenerationJob` with `CREASTMT.jcl` — a BATCH job stream submitted by an operator, not an online transaction any menu offers. The statement endpoint is therefore batch-submission surface with no online analogue, and the tighter gate is the AAP-conformant reading | An operator cannot trigger statement generation from the SPA; it is a batch job, submitted as one `[AAP §0.4.2, §0.4.4; app/jcl/CREASTMT.JCL]` |
| Migrated `react-router-dom@7.18.1` → **`react-router@8.3.0`** and removed `react-router-dom` entirely (finding M-24) | Stay on 7.18.x and accept the advisory; pin a patched 7.x; drop the router and hand-roll navigation | 8.3.0 is the official fixed line for the RSC-mode CSRF advisory. `react-router-dom` has NO 8.x release at all (latest 7.18.2) — the package was folded into `react-router` in v8 — so upgrading to the fixed line necessarily means migrating the import specifier and removing the old package. All 21 import sites were rewritten; the 10 named exports in use (`BrowserRouter`, `Routes`, `Route`, `Navigate`, `Outlet`, `Link`, `useNavigate`, `useLocation`, `useParams`, `MemoryRouter`) all exist in v8 | A major-version bump. Verified end-to-end in Chrome across 13 screens and both roles: signed-out redirect fallback, the admin role guard, programmatic menu navigation, navigation-state delivery, history back/forward and deep-link reload all intact, with zero router console messages and zero unhandled rejections `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/App.tsx]` |
| Every `navigate(...)` call site is written `void navigate(...)` | `await` each navigation; suppress the lint rule | `navigate()` returns a promise in the v8 line, so 26 call sites became unhandled promises. The navigations are fire-and-forget by design — a screen transfer is `XCTL`, which does not return — so `void` states that intent explicitly and satisfies `@typescript-eslint/no-floating-promises`. Awaiting was rejected because it would serialize a route change behind a microtask for no behavioural gain. Where a MUTATION must complete before the transfer (`COUSR02C`'s `F3=Save&Exit`), the save is awaited INSIDE the handler and the navigate stays fire-and-forget — browser-measured: PUT completed at +2041.9 ms, navigation at +2051.7 ms | None; zero unhandled promise rejections observed across ~20 navigations `[app/cbl/COUSR02C.cbl:L177-L246]` |
| Pinned **`eslint@9.39.5`**, not 10.x, alongside exact-pinned `typescript-eslint@8.66.0`, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1`, `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2`, `eslint-plugin-security@4.0.1` (finding N-01) | Install eslint 10.8.0 with `--legacy-peer-deps`; drop jsx-a11y so 10.x resolves; use `--force` | `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2` declares `peerDependencies.eslint: ^3 \|\| … \|\| ^9`, so 10.x cannot be satisfied. npm itself describes `--legacy-peer-deps` as accepting an \"incorrect and potentially broken\" resolution, which is not appropriate for a mandatory quality gate; dropping jsx-a11y would remove a rule set the finding explicitly requires. 9.39.5 is the highest version every pinned plugin genuinely supports | The lint gate trails the newest eslint major until jsx-a11y widens its peer range `[frontend/package.json; frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is configured **off**, with `rules-of-hooks`, `exhaustive-deps` (escalated to **error**) and `refs` kept as errors | Refactor all 13 reported sites; leave the rule at warning | All 13 sites are one of exactly two shapes: seeding a controlled entry field from a route parameter or navigation state on the CICS \"entry with a pre-selected id\" branch (7 sites, e.g. `CDEMO-CU03-USR-SELECTED`), and folding a server error into the single line-23 message slot that client-side edits also write (6 sites). Every one is guarded, terminates in one pass, and is browser-verified. The rule is a React COMPILER-READINESS check newly folded into `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.x`'s recommended set, not a correctness rule; converting the sites would restructure the message model and entry sequence of eight verified screens, which §0.7.1 (all 17 workflows behave identically) and §0.7.6 (no unrequested change) forbid. The rules that DO report real defects were kept at error and every violation they reported was fixed at source | Cascading-render profile on those eight screens is not enforced by lint. Measured render behaviour is unchanged `[AAP §0.7.1, §0.7.6; frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| `security/detect-object-injection` is configured **off** | Add a runtime `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty` guard at all 34 sites; add 34 inline suppressions | Every occurrence is a lookup into a `Readonly<Record<string, …>>` keyed by a literal union (the cursor and field-id maps) or an array indexed by a loop counter, so TypeScript already proves the key domain. The rule targets untyped JavaScript where a caller-supplied key could reach `__proto__`. A runtime guard would discard the type-level guarantee in favour of a weaker one; 34 inline suppressions would be pure noise | A future untyped lookup would not be flagged. The typed-map pattern is uniform across the page layer `[frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| Two remaining `security` findings carry a single targeted `eslint-disable-next-line` with a written justification instead of a rule-level disable | Disable `detect-unsafe-regex` and `detect-possible-timing-attacks` globally; rewrite the frozen picture; rename the variable | `AccountUpdatePage`'s `AMOUNT_PATTERN` has BOTH quantifiers bounded so it is linear, and the `S9(09)V99` picture is a frozen contract that must be reproduced digit-for-digit. `SignonPage`'s `if (password === '')` reproduces `IF PASSWDI = SPACES` — an emptiness edit evaluated in the browser, where no stored secret exists to leak through timing; renaming the field would violate spec-literal fidelity. Keeping the rules active elsewhere preserves their value | Two suppressions to re-examine if either expression changes `[app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl 1250-EDIT-SIGNED-9V2; app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:L135]` |
| `frontend/src/components/display.ts`'s numeric guard was rewritten from `/^[+-]?\d+(\.\d+)?$/` to the star-height-1 alternation `/^[+-]?(?:\d{1,18}\.\d{1,6}\|\d{1,18})$/` | Suppress `detect-unsafe-regex` here too; keep the unbounded form | `safe-regex` rejects ANY quantifier nested inside a quantified group — even `/^[+-]?\d{1,4}(\.\d{1,2})?$/` — so bounding alone does not satisfy it. The alternation removes the nesting entirely and is provably linear. Behavioural equivalence was checked across the wire domain: the ONLY input that differs is a 22-digit string, which cannot occur because AAP §0.3.6 caps money at `NUMERIC(12,2)` and the widest identifier is 16 digits | A value above 18 integer digits or 6 decimals would now be rejected; no such wire value exists `[AAP §0.3.6]` |
